He'll go on and on about his swing, or some tournament on TV, and the announcers are always whispering. He actually went to the Masters once, years ago. Said it was the greatest experience of his life. He always talks about how they sell those pimento cheese sandwiches for like a dollar fifty. You really can't beat those prices at a sporting event nowadays, you know? You go to a baseball game now and a standard hot dog is twelve bucks, and a draft beer is even worse. It's highway robbery. Used to be you could take the whole family out to the ballpark for under fifty bucks, get some peanuts, keep score in the little paper program... \n\nBut, uh, anyway... boring topics. Yeah, I honestly just couldn't tell you if I can focus on them or not. I'm sorry, I just haven't noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty following conversations is because you're not interested in what's being said?\n\nPatient: I really don't know, Doc. I mean, maybe? I just haven't paid enough attention to whether it's the interest level or something else. I just don't have a clue. \n\nIt makes me think of when my wife tries to talk to me about her book club. She reads these huge historical fiction novels. Last month they read one about... I think it was the French Revolution? Or maybe the Russian one. I don't know, it had a lot of snow in it, from what she was saying. She gets these books from the library downtown. Have you seen what they did to the library? They completely remodeled the front entrance. Used to be these nice heavy wooden doors, real classic looking, and now it's all glass and steel. Looks like a spaceship landed in the middle of Main Street. I was talking to the head librarian, Mrs. Higgins—she's been there since I was taking my kids to story hour—and she said the heating bill has gone through the roof because of all that glass. They had to put these ugly space heaters behind the circulation desk just to keep their toes warm in the winter...\n\nBut, yeah, about the conversations and being interested... I honestly couldn't tell you if that's the reason. I just haven't kept track of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have trouble following conversations if you were highly motivated to understand what was being said?\n\nPatient: I mean, highly motivated... I guess that's another one I just don't have a good answer for. I haven't really noticed if motivation changes anything for me. \n\nLike, what does highly motivated even mean, really? Like if there was money on the line? My neighbor, Gary, he's always trying to get me in on his poker nights. He takes it so seriously, you'd think we were playing in Vegas. He bought this custom felt table online, had it shipped all the way from Nevada. Cost him a pretty penny, too. The shipping alone was astronomical because it's so heavy. But Gary, he's got that kind of disposable income since he retired from the auto plant. He was a supervisor over there for thirty years. They gave him a gold watch and everything when he left. You don't see companies doing that anymore, taking care of their people like that. Now it's just a cheap cake in the breakroom and a handshake if you're lucky. My brother worked for a paper company for twenty years and all they gave him was a framed certificate that they spelled his name wrong on...\n\nBut yeah, about following conversations if I'm motivated... I really couldn't tell you. I just haven't paid attention to whether it makes a difference or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty following conversations is always because you're not interested in what's being said?\n\nPatient: Is it *always* because I'm not interested? Gosh, Doc, I feel like I'm striking out today. I really just don't know. I haven't kept a log of why I lose the thread of what people are saying. \n\nI mean, who is interested in everything, anyway? Like my brother-in-law, Dave. He was just over here Sunday talking my ear off about his new RV. He bought one of those massive Class A motorhomes, the ones that look like a rock band's tour bus. He was going on and on about the water filtration system and the black water tank... I mean, who wants to hear about a portable septic tank? But he loves it. He parked that monstrosity right in his driveway, and let me tell you, the HOA threw an absolute fit. You know how those Homeowner Associations are, right? A bunch of busybodies. They sent him a certified letter on this fancy heavy-stock paper threatening to fine him fifty dollars a day. Fifty dollars! Just for parking a vehicle on his own property. It's completely ridiculous. I told him he should take it up with the city council, because the local zoning laws actually say if it's on a paved surface and not blocking the sidewalk... \n\nBut, uh, what were we... right, being interested. Yeah, I just couldn't tell you if that's the only reason I have trouble following along. I honestly haven't noticed the exact reason why it happens."
  }
]
